| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | No, noble mistress, ’tis fresh morning with me<br>When you are by at [[motifs/night\|night]]. I do beseech you,<br>Chiefly that I might set it in my prayers,<br>What is your name? |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]].<br>O my father,<br>I have broke your best to say so! |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | Admired [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]]!<br>Indeed the top of admiration, worth<br>What’s dearest to the world. Full many a lady<br>I have eyed with best regard, and many a [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Th’harmony of their tongues hath into bondage<br>Brought my too diligent ear. For several virtues<br>Have I liked several women; never any<br>With so full [[motifs/soul\|soul]] but some defect in her<br>Did quarrel with the noblest grace she owed<br>And put it to the foil. But you, O you,<br>So perfect and so peerless, are created<br>Of every creature’s best. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | I do not know<br>One of my sex, no woman’s face remember<br>Save from my glass mine own; nor have I seen<br>More that I may call men than you, good friend,<br><br>And my dear father. How features are abroad<br>I am skilless of; but, by my modesty,<br>The [[motifs/jewel\|jewel]] in my dower, I would not wish<br>Any companion in the world but you;<br>Nor can [[concepts/imagination\|imagination]] form a shape<br>Besides yourself to like of. But I prattle<br>Something too wildly, and my father’s precepts<br>I therein do forget. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | I am in my condition<br>A prince, [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]], I do think a [[archetypes/king\|king]]—<br>I would not so—and would no more endure<br>This wooden [[concepts/slavery\|slavery]] than to suffer<br>The flesh-fly blow my mouth. Hear my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] speak.<br>The very instant that I saw you did<br>My heart fly to your service; there resides<br>To make me slave to it. And for your sake<br>Am I this patient log-man. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | Do you [[motifs/love\|love]] me? |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | O heaven, O earth, bear witness to this sound,<br>And [[motifs/crown\|crown]] what I profess with kind event<br>If I speak true! If hollowly, invert<br>What best is boded me to mischief!<br>Beyond all limit of what else i’th’ world,<br>Do [[motifs/love\|love]], prize, [[concepts/honour\|honour]] you. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | (weeping)<br>I am a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]<br>To weep at what I am glad of. |
| PROSPERO | (aside)<br>Fair encounter<br>Of two most rare affections! Heavens rain grace<br>On that which breeds between ’em. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | (to [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]])<br>Wherefore weep you? |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | At mine unworthiness, that dare not offer<br>What I [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to give, and much less take<br>What I shall die to want. But this is trifling,<br>And all the more it seeks to hide itself<br>The bigger bulk it shows. Hence, bashful cunning,<br>And prompt me, plain and holy innocence.<br>I am your wife, if you will marry me.<br>If not, I’ll die your maid. To be your fellow<br>You may deny me, but I’ll be your servant<br>Whether you will or no. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] kneeling |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | My mistress, dearest;<br>And I thus humble ever. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | My husband then? |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | Ay, with a heart as willing<br>As bondage e’er of [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]]. Here’s my hand. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | And mine, with my heart in’t. And now farewell<br>Till half an hour hence. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | A thousand thousand. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] and [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] |
| PROSPERO | So glad of this as they I cannot be,<br>Who are surprised with all; but my rejoicing<br>At nothing can be more. I’ll to my book,<br>For yet ere supper-[[motifs/time\|time]] must I perform<br>Much business appertaining. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]], Stefano, and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]] |
| STEFANO | (to [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) Tell not me. When the butt is out we will drink [[motifs/water\|water]], not a drop before. Therefore bear up and board ’em. Servant [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], drink to me. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Servant [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]? The folly of this island! They say there’s but five upon this isle. We are three of them; if th’other two be brained like us, the state totters. |
| STEFANO | Drink, servant [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], when I bid thee. Thy eyes are almost set in thy head. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Where should they be set else? He were a brave [[archetypes/monster\|monster]] indeed if they were set in his tail. |
| STEFANO | My man-[[archetypes/monster\|monster]] hath drowned his tongue in sack. For my part, the sea cannot drown me. I swam, ere I could recover the shore, five and thirty leagues, off and on. By this light, thou shalt be my lieutenant, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], or my standard. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Your lieutenant if you list; he’s no standard. |
| STEFANO | We’ll not run, Monsieur [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]]. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Nor go neither; but you’ll lie like dogs, and yet say nothing neither. |
| STEFANO | [[motifs/moon\|Moon]]-calf, speak once in thy life, if thou beest a good [[motifs/moon\|moon]]-calf. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | How does thy [[concepts/honour\|honour]]? Let me lick thy shoe.<br>I’ll not serve him; he is not valiant. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Thou liest, most ignorant [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]! I am in case to jostle a constable. Why, thou debauched fish, thou, was there ever man a coward that hath drunk so much sack as I today? Wilt thou tell a monstrous lie, being but half a fish and half a [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]? |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | (to Stefano) Lo, how he mocks me! Wilt thou let him, my lord? |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | ‘Lord’ quoth he? That a [[archetypes/monster\|monster]] should be such a natural! |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | (to Stefano) Lo, lo, again! Bite him to [[motifs/death\|death]], I prithee. |
| STEFANO | [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], keep a good tongue in your head. If you prove a mutineer, the next tree. The poor [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]’s my subject, and he shall not suffer indignity. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | I thank my noble lord. Wilt thou be pleased<br>To hearken once again to the suit I made to thee? |
| STEFANO | Marry, will I. Kneel and repeat it. I will stand, and so shall [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]] kneels. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], invisible |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | As I told thee before, I am subject to a [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]], a sorcerer, that by his cunning hath cheated me of the island. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Thou liest. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | (to [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]])<br>Thou liest, thou jesting monkey, thou.<br>I would my valiant master would destroy thee.<br>I do not lie. |
| STEFANO | [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], if you trouble him any more in’s tale, by this hand, I will supplant some of your teeth. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Why, I said nothing. |
| STEFANO | Mum, then, and no more. (To [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) Proceed. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | I say by sorcery he got this isle;<br>From me he got it. If thy [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] will<br>[[concepts/revenge\|Revenge]] it on him—for I know thou dar’st,<br>But this thing dare not— |
| STEFANO | That’s most certain. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Thou shalt be lord of it, and I’ll serve thee. |
| STEFANO | How now shall this be compassed? Canst thou bring me to the party? |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Yea, yea, my lord. I’ll yield him thee asleep<br>Where thou mayst knock a nail into his head. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Thou liest, thou canst not. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | What a pied ninny’s thisl (To Tririculo) Thou scurvy<br>patch!<br>(To Stefano) I do beseech thy [[concepts/greatness\|greatness]] give him blows,<br>And take his bottle from him. When that’s gone<br>He shall drink naught but brine, for I’ll not show him<br>Where the quick freshes are. |
| STEFANO | [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], run into no further danger. Interrupt the [[archetypes/monster\|monster]] one word further, and, by this hand, I’ll turn my [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] out o’doors and make a stockfish of thee. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Why, what did I? I did nothing. I’ll go farther off. |
| STEFANO | Didst thou not say he lied? |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Thou liest. |
| STEFANO | Do I so? (Striking [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]) Take thou that. As you like this, give me the lie another [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | I did not give the lie. Out o‘your wits and hearing too? A pox o’your bottle! This can sack and drinking do. A murrain on your [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], and the devil take your fingers. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Ha, ha, ha! |
| STEFANO | Now forward with your tale. (To [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]) Prithee, stand further off. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Beat him enough; after a little [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I’ll beat him too. |
| STEFANO | (to [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]])<br>Stand farther. (To [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) Come, proceed. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Why, as I told thee, ’tis a custom with him<br>I’th’ afternoon to [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. There thou mayst brain him,<br>Having first seized his books; or with a log<br>Batter his skull, or paunch him with a stake,<br>Or cut his weasand with thy knife. Remember<br>First to possess his books, for without them<br>He’s but a sot as I am, nor hath not<br>One spirit to command—they all do hate him<br>As rootedly as I. Burn but his books.<br>He has brave utensils, for so he calls them,<br>Which when he has a house he’ll deck withal.<br>And that most deeply to consider is<br>The [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]] of his daughter. He himself<br>Calls her a nonpareil. I never saw a woman<br>But only Sycorax my dam and she,<br>But she as far surpasseth Sycorax<br>As great’st does least. |
| STEFANO | Is it so brave a lass? |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Ay, lord. She will become thy bed, I warrant,<br>And bring thee forth brave brood. |
| STEFANO | [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]], I will kill this man. His daughter and I will be [[archetypes/king\|king]] and [[characters/queen\|queen]]—save our graces!—and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]] and thyself shall be viceroys. Dost thou like the plot, [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]? no |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Excellent. |
| STEFANO | Give me thy hand. I am sorry I beat thee. But while thou liv’st, keep a good tongue in thy head. |
| *(didascalia)* | Within this half hour will he be asleep. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Wilt thou destroy him then? |
| STEFANO | Ay, on mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | (aside) This will I tell my master. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Thou mak’st me merry; I am full of pleasure.<br>Let us be jocund. Will you troll the catch<br>You taught me but while-ere? |
| STEFANO | At thy request, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], I will do reason, any reason.—Come on, [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], let us sing.<br>(Sings)<br>Flout ’em and cout ’em, And scout ’em and flout ’em.<br>Thought is free. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | That’s not the tune.<br>[[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] plays the tune on a tabor and pipe |
| STEFANO | What is this same? |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | This is the tune of our catch, played by the picture of Nobody. |
| STEFANO | (calls towards [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]) If thou beest a man, show thyself in thy likeness. If thou beest a devil, take’t as thou list. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | O, forgive me my sins! |
| STEFANO | He that dies pays all debts. (Calls) I defy thee.—[[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]] upon us! |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Art thou afeard? |
| STEFANO | No, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], not I. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Be not afeard. The isle is full of noises, |
| *(didascalia)* | Sounds, and sweet airs, that give delight and hurt |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | not.<br>Sometimes a thousand twangling instruments<br>Will hum about mine ears, and sometime voices<br>That if I then had waked after long [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]<br>Will make me [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] again; and then in dreaming<br>The clouds methought would open and show riches<br>Ready to drop upon me, that when I waked<br>I cried to dream again. |
| STEFANO | This will prove a brave kingdom to me, where I shall have my music for nothing. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | When Prospero is destroyed. |
| STEFANO | That shall be by and by. I remember the story. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], playing music |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | The sound is going away. Let’s follow it, and after do our work. |
| STEFANO | Lead, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]; we’ll follow.—I would I could see this taborer. He lays it on. |
| TRICULO | (to [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) Wilt come? I’ll follow Stefano. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]], [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]], Adrian, and Francisco |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | (to [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]])<br>By’r la’kin, I can go no further, sir.<br>My old bones ache. Here’s a maze trod indeed<br>Through forthrights and meanders. By your patience,<br>I needs must rest me. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Old lord, I cannot blame thee,<br>Who am myself attached with weariness<br>To th’ dulling of my spirits. Sit down and rest.<br>Even here I will put off my [[concepts/hope\|hope]], and keep it<br>No longer for my flatterer. He is drowned<br>Whom thus we stray to find, and the sea mocks<br>Our frustrate search on land. [[motifs/well\|Well]], let him go. |
| *(didascalia)* | They sit |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | (aside to [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]])<br>I am right glad that he’s so out of [[concepts/hope\|hope]].<br>Do not for one repulse forgo the purpose<br>That you resolved t’effect. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | (aside to [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) The next advantage<br>Will we take throughly. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | (aside to [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]]) Let it be tonight,<br>For now they are oppressed with travel. They<br>Will not nor cannot use such vigilance<br>As when they are fresh. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | (aside to [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) I say tonight. No more.<br>Solemn and strange music. Enter Prospero on the top, invisible |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | What harmony is this? My good friends, hark. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Marvellous sweet music. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter spirits, in several strange shapes, bringing in a table and a banquet, and [[motifs/dance\|dance]] about it with gentle actions of salutations, and, inviting the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and his companions to eat, they depart |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Give us kind keepers, heavens! What were these? |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | A living drollery. Now I will believe<br>That there are unicorns; that in Arabia<br>There is one tree, the phoenix’ throne, one phoenix<br>At this hour reigning there. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | I’ll believe both;<br>And what does else want credit come to me,<br>And I’ll be sworn ’tis true. Travellers ne’er did lie,<br>Though fools at [[motifs/home\|home]] condemn ’em. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | If in Naples<br>I should report this now, would they believe me—<br>If I should say I saw such islanders?<br>For certes these are people of the island,<br>Who though they are of monstrous shape, yet note<br>Their manners are more gentle-kind than of<br>Our human generation you shall find<br>Many, nay, almost any. |
| PROSPERO | (aside)<br>Honest lord,<br>Thou hast said [[motifs/well\|well]], for some of you there present<br>Are worse than devils. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | I cannot too much muse.<br>Such shapes, such gesture, and such sound,<br>expressing—<br>Although they want the use of tongue—a kind<br>Of excellent dumb discourse. |
| PROSPERO | (aside)<br>Praise in departing. |
| FRANCISCO | They vanished strangely. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | No matter, since<br>They have left their viands behind, for we have<br>stomachs.<br>Will’t please you taste of what is here? |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Not I. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], sir, you need not [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. When we were boys,<br>Who would believe that there were mountaineers<br>Dewlapped like bulls, whose throats had hanging at ’em<br>Wallets of flesh? Or that there were such men<br>Whose heads stood in their breasts? Which now we<br>find<br>Each putter-out of five for one will bring us<br>Good warrant of. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] rising I will stand to and feed, |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Although my last—no matter, since I feel<br>The best is past. Brother, my lord the Duke,<br>Stand to, and do as we. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] approach the table. Thunder and [[motifs/lightning\|lightning]]. [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] descends like a harpy, claps his wings upon the table, and, with a quaint device, the banquet vanishes |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | You are three men of sin, whom destiny—<br>That hath to instrument this lower world<br>And what is in’t—the never-surfeited sea<br>Hath caused to belch up you, and on this island<br>Where man doth not inhabit, you ’mongst men<br>Being most unfit to live. I have made you mad,<br>And even with suchlike valour men hang and drown<br>Their proper selves.<br>[[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] draw<br>You fools! I and my fellows<br>Are ministers of fate. The elements<br>Of whom your swords are tempered may as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>Wound the loud winds, or with bemocked-at stabs<br>Kill the still-closing waters, as diminish<br>One dowl that’s in my plume. My fellow ministers<br>Are like invulnerable. If you could hurt,<br>Your swords are now too massy for your strengths<br>And will not be uplifted.<br>[[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] stand amazed<br>But remember,<br>For that’s my business to you, that you three<br>From Milan did supplant good Prospero;<br>Exposed unto the sea, which hath requit it,<br>Him and his [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]] [[archetypes/child\|child]]; for which foul deed,<br>The powers, delaying not forgetting, have<br>Incensed the seas and shores, yea, all the creatures,<br>Against your peace. Thee of thy son, [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]],<br>They have bereft, and do pronounce by me<br>Ling’ring perdition—worse than any [[motifs/death\|death]]<br>Can be at once—shatt step by step attend<br>You and your ways; whose wraths to guard you<br>from—<br>Which here in this most desolate isle else falls<br>Upon your heads—is nothing but heart’s sorrow<br>And a clear life ensuing. |
| *(didascalia)* | He ascends and vanishes in thunder. Then, to soft music, enter the spirits again, and [[motifs/dance\|dance]] with mocks and mows, and they depart, carrying out the table |
| PROSPERO | Bravely the figure of this harpy hast thou<br>Performed, my [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]; a grace it had devouring.<br>Of my instruction hast thou nothing bated<br>In what thou hadst to say. So with good life<br>And observation strange my meaner ministers<br>Their several kinds have done. My high charms work,<br>And these mine enemies are all knit up<br>In their distractions. They now are in my power;<br>And in these fits I leave them, while I visit<br>Young [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]], whom they suppose is drowned,<br>And his and mine loved darling. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]], Adrian, and Francisco go towards the others |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | I’th’ name of something holy, sir, why stand you<br>In this strange stare? |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | O, it is monstrous, monstrous!<br>Methought the billows spoke and told me of it,<br>The winds did sing it to me, and the thunder,<br>That deep and dreadful organ-pipe, pronounced<br>The name of Prosper. It did bass my trespass.<br>Therefor my son i’th’ ooze is bedded, and<br>I’ll seek him deeper than e’er plummet sounded,<br>And with him there lie mudded. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | But one fiend at a [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>I’ll fight their legions o’er. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | I’ll be thy second. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | All three of them are desperate. Their great [[concepts/guilt\|guilt]],<br>Like [[motifs/poison\|poison]] given to work a great [[motifs/time\|time]] after,<br>Now ’gins to bite the spirits. I do beseech you<br>That are of suppler joints, follow them swiftly,<br>And hinder them from what this ecstasy<br>May now provoke them to. |
| ADRIAN | Follow, I pray you. Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prospero, [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]], and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] |
| PROSPFRO | (to [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]])<br>If I have too austerely punished you,<br>Your compensation makes amends, for I<br>Have given you here a third of mine own tife—<br>Or that for which I live—who once again<br>I tender to thy hand. All thy vexations<br>Were but my trials of thy [[motifs/love\|love]], and thou<br>Hast strangely stood the test. Here, afore heaven,<br>I ratify this my rich gift. O [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]],<br>Do not smile at me that I boast of her,<br>For thou shalt find she will outstrip all praise,<br>And make it halt behind her. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | I do believe it<br>Against an oracle. |
| PROSPERO | Then, as my gift and thine own acquisition<br>Worthily purchased, take my daughter. But<br>If thou dost break her virgin-knot before<br>All sanctimonious ceremonies may<br>With full and holy rite be ministered,<br>No sweet aspersion shall the heavens let fall<br>To make this contract grow; but barren hate,<br>Sour-eyed disdain, and discord, shall bestrew<br>The [[concepts/union\|union]] of your bed with weeds so loathly<br>That you shall hate it both. Therefore take heed,<br>As Hymen’s lamps shall light you. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | As I [[concepts/hope\|hope]]<br>For quiet days, fair issue, and long life<br>With such [[motifs/love\|love]] as ’tis now, the murkiest den,<br>The most opportune place, the strong’st suggestion<br>Our worser genius can, shall never melt<br>Mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] into lust to take away<br>The edge of that day’s celebration;<br>When I shall think or Phoebus’ steeds are foundered<br>Or [[motifs/night\|night]] kept chained below. |
| PROSPERO | Fairly spoke.<br>Sit, then, and talk with her. She is thine own.<br>[[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] sit and talk together<br>What, [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], my industrious servant [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | What would my potent master? Here I am. |
| PROSPERO | Thou and thy meaner fellows your last service<br>Did worthily perform, and I must use you<br>In such another trick. Go bring the rabble,<br>O’er whom I give thee power, here to this place.<br>Incite them to quick motion, for I must<br>Bestow upon the eyes of this young couple<br>Some [[concepts/vanity\|vanity]] of mine art. It is my promise,<br>And they expect it from me. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Presently? |
| PROSPERO | Ay, with a twink. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Before you can say ‘Come’ and ‘Go’, And breathe twice, and cry ‘So, so’,<br>Each one tripping on his toe<br>Will be here with mop and mow.<br>Do you [[motifs/love\|love]] me, master? No? |
| PROSPERO | Dearly, my delicate [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]. Do not approach<br>Till thou dost hear me call. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]]; I conceive. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| PROSPERO | (to [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]])<br>Look thou be true. Do not give dalliance<br>Too much the rein. The strongest oaths are straw<br>To th’ [[motifs/fire\|fire]] i’th’ [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. Be more abstemious,<br>Or else, good [[motifs/night\|night]] your vow. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | I warrant you, sir,<br>The white cold virgin [[motifs/snow\|snow]] upon my heart<br>Abates the ardour of my liver. |
| PROSPERO | [[motifs/well\|Well]].—<br>Now come, my [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]! Bring a corollary<br>Rather than want a spirit. Appear, and pertly.<br>Soft music<br>(To [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]]) No tongue, all eyes! Be silent. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Iris |
| IRIS | Ceres, most bounteous lady, thy rich leas<br>Of wheat, rye, barley, vetches, oats, and peas;<br>Thy turfy [[settings/mountains\|mountains]] where live nibbling sheep,<br>And flat meads thatched with stover, them to keep;<br>Thy banks with peonied and twillèd brims<br>Which spongy April at thy hest betrims<br>To make cold nymphs chaste crowns; and thy broom-groves,<br>Whose [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]] the dismissed bachelor loves,<br>Being lass-lorn; thy pole-clipped vineyard,<br>And thy sea-marge, sterile and rocky-hard,<br>Where thou thyself dost air: the [[characters/queen\|Queen]] o’th’ Sky,<br>Whose wat’ry arch and messenger am I,<br>Bids thee leave these, and with her sovereign grace |
| *(didascalia)* | Juno appears in the air |
| IRIS | Here on this grass-plot, in this very place,<br>To come and sport.—Her peacocks fly amain.<br>Approach, rich Ceres, her to entertain. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] as Ceres |
| CERES | Hail, many-coloured messenger, that ne’er<br>Dost disobey the wife of Jupiter;<br>Who with thy saffron wings upon my [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]]<br>Diffusest honey-drops, refreshing showers,<br>And with each end of thy blue bow dost [[motifs/crown\|crown]]<br>My bosky acres and my unshrubbed down,<br>Rich scarf to my proud earth. Why hath thy [[characters/queen\|queen]]<br>Summoned me hither to this short-grassed green? |
| IRIS | A contract of true [[motifs/love\|love]] to celebrate,<br>And some donation freely to estate<br>On the blest lovers. |
| CERES | Tell me, heavenly bow,<br>If [[characters/venus\|Venus]] or her son, as thou dost know,<br>Do now attend the [[characters/queen\|Queen]]. Since they did plot<br>The means that dusky Dis my daughter got,<br>Her and her blind boy’s scandalled company<br>I have forsworn. |
| IRIS | Of her society<br>Be not afraid. I met her deity<br>Cutting the clouds towards Paphos, and her son<br>Dove-drawn with her. Here thought they to have<br>done<br>Some wanton charm upon this man and maid,<br>Whose vows are that no bed-right shall be paid<br>Till Hymen’s torch be lighted—but in vain.<br>Mars’s hot minion is returned again.<br>Her waspish-headed son has broke his arrows,<br>Swears he will shoot no more, but play with<br>sparrows,<br>And be a boy right out. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music. Juno descends to the stage |
| CERES | Highest [[characters/queen\|queen]] of state,<br>Great Juno, comes; I know her by her gait. |
| JUNO | How does my bounteous sister? Go with me<br>To bless this twain, that they may prosperous be,<br>And honoured in their issue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Ceres joins Juno, and they sing |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | This is a most majestic vision, and<br>Harmonious charmingly. May I be bold<br>To think these spirits? |
| PROSPERO | Spirits, which by mine art<br>I have from their confines called to enact<br>My present fancies. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | Let me live here ever!<br>So rare a wondered father and a wise<br>Makes this place paradise.<br>Juno and Ceres whisper, and send Iris on employment |
| PROSPERO | Sweet now, [[motifs/silence\|silence]].<br>Juno and Ceres whisper seriously.<br>There’s something else to do. Hush, and be mute,<br>Or else our spell is marred. |
| IRIS | You nymphs called naiads of the [[motifs/wind\|wind]]’ring brooks,<br>With your sedged crowns and ever-harmless looks,<br>Leave your crisp channels, and on this green land<br>Answer your summons; Juno does command.<br>Come, temperate nymphs, and help to celebrate<br>A contract of true [[motifs/love\|love]]. Be not too late. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter certain nymphs |
| IRIS | You sunburned sicklemen, of August weary,<br>Come hither from the furrow and be merry;<br>Make holiday, your rye-straw hats put on,<br>And these fresh nymphs encounter every one<br>In country footing. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter certain reapers, properly habited. They join with the nymphs in a graceful [[motifs/dance\|dance]]; towards the end whereof Prospero starts suddenly, and speaks |
| PROSPERO | (aside)<br>I had forgot that foul conspiracy<br>Of the beast [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]] and his confederates<br>Against my life. The minute of their plot<br>Is almost come. (To the spirits) [[motifs/well\|Well]] done! Avoid; no<br>more! |
| *(didascalia)* | To a strange, hollow, and confused noise, the spirits in the pageant heavily vanish. [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] rise |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | (to [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]])<br>This is strange. Your father’s in some passion<br>That works him strongly. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | Never till this day<br>Saw I him touched with anger so distempered. |
| PROSPERO | You do look, my son, in a moved sort,<br>As if you were dismayed. Be cheerful, sir.<br>Our revels now are ended. These our actors,<br>As I foretold you, were all spirits, and<br>Are melted into air, into thin air;<br>And like the baseless fabric of this vision,<br>The cloud-capped towers, the gorgeous palaces,<br>The solemn temples, the great globe itself,<br>Yea, all which it inherit, shall dissolve;<br>And, like this insubstantial pageant faded,<br>Leave not a rack behind. We are such stuff<br>As [[motifs/dreams\|dreams]] are made on, and our little life<br>Is rounded with a [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. Sir, I am vexed.<br>Bear with my weakness. My old brain is troubled.<br>Be not disturbed with my infirmity.<br>If you be pleased, retire into my cell,<br>And there repose. A turn or two I’ll walk<br>To still my beating mind. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | and [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]]<br>We wish your peace. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] |
| PROSPERO | Come with a thought! I thank thee, [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]. Come! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Thy thoughts I cleave to. What’s thy pleasure? |
| PROSPERO | Spirit,<br>We must prepare to meet with [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Ay, my commander. When I presented Ceres<br>I thought to have told thee of it, but I feared<br>Lest I might anger thee. |
| PROSPERO | Say again: where didst thou leave these varlets? |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | I told you, sir, they were red-hot with drinking;<br>So full of valour that they smote the air<br>For breathing in their faces, beat the ground<br>For kissing of their feet; yet always bending<br>Towards their project. Then I beat my tabor,<br>At which like unbacked colts they pricked their ears,<br>Advanced their eyelids, lifted up their noses<br>As they smelt music. So I charmed their ears<br>That calf-like they my lowing followed, through<br>Toothed briars, sharp furzes, pricking gorse, and<br>thorns,<br>Which entered their frail shins. At last I left them<br>I’th’ filthy-mantled pool beyond your cell,<br>There dancing up to th’ chins, that the foul lake<br>O’er-stunk their feet. |
| PROSPERO | This was [[motifs/well\|well]] done, my bird.<br>Thy shape invisible retain thou still.<br>The trumpery in my house, go bring it hither<br>For stale to catch these thieves. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | I go, I go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| PROSPERO | A devil, a born devil, on whose [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Nurture can never stick; on whom my pains,<br>Humanely taken, all, all lost, quite lost,<br>And, as with age his [[concepts/body\|body]] uglier grows,<br>So his mind cankers. I will plague them all,<br>Even to roaring. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], laden with glistening apparel, etc. |
| PROSPERO | Come, hang them on this lime. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] hangs up the apparel. Exeunt Prospero and |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]], Stefano, and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], all wet |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Pray you, tread softly, that the blind mole may<br>Not hear a foot fall. We now are near his cell. |
| STEFANO | [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]], your fairy, which you say is a harmless fairy, has done little better than played the Jack with us. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]], I do smell all [[motifs/horse\|horse]]-piss, at which my nose is in great indignation. |
| STEFANO | So is mine. Do you hear, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]? If I should take a displeasure against you, look you— |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Thou wert but a lost [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Good my lord, give me thy favour still.<br>Be patient, for the prize I’ll bring thee to<br>Shall hoodwink this mischance. Therefore speak softly.<br>All’s hushed as midnight yet. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Ay, but to lose our bottles in the pool! |
| STEFANO | There is not only disgrace and dishonour in that, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], but an infinite loss. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | That’s more to me than my wetting. Yet this is your harmless fairy, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]]. |
| STEFANO | I will fetch off my bottle, though I be o’er ears for my labour. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Prithee, my [[archetypes/king\|king]], be quiet. Seest thou here;<br>This is the mouth o’th’ cell. No noise, and enter.<br>Do that good mischief which may make this island<br>Thine own for ever, and I thy [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]<br>For aye thy foot-licker. |
| STEFANO | Give me thy hand.<br>I do begin to have bloody thoughts. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | (seeing the apparel) O [[archetypes/king\|King]] Stefano, O peer! O worthy Stefano, look what a wardrobe here is for thee! |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Let it alone, thou [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], it is but trash. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | (putting on a gown) O ho, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]], we know what belongs to a frippery! O [[archetypes/king\|King]] Stefano! |
| STEFANO | Put off that gown, [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]. By this hand, I’ll have that gown. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Thy grace shall have it. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | The dropsy drown this [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]! What do you mean<br>To dote thus on such luggage? Let’t alone,<br>And do the murder first. If he awake,<br>From toe to [[motifs/crown\|crown]] he’ll fill our skins with pinches,<br>Make us strange stuff. |
| STEFANO | Be you quiet, [[archetypes/monster\|monster]].—Mistress lime, is not this my jerkin? Now is the jerkin under the line. Now, jerkin, you are like to lose your hair and prove a bald jerkin.<br>Stefano and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]] take garments |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | Do, do! We steal by line and level, an’t like your grace. |
| STEFANO | I thank thee for that jest. Here’s a garment for’t. Wit shall not go unrewarded while I am [[archetypes/king\|king]] of this country. ‘Steal by line and level’ is an excellent pass of pate. There’s another garment for’t. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]], come, put some lime upon your fingers, and away with the rest. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | I will have none on’t. We shall lose our [[motifs/time\|time]],<br>And all be turned to barnacles, or to apes<br>With foreheads villainous low. |
| STEFANO | [[archetypes/monster\|Monster]], lay to your fingers. Help to bear this away where my hogshead of wine is, or I’ll turn you out of my kingdom. Go to, carry this. |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | And this. |
| STEFANO | Ay, and this.<br>They load [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]] with apparel. A noise of hunters heard. Enter divers spirits in shape of dogs and hounds, hunting them about; Prospero and [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] setting them on |
| PROSPERO | Hey, Mountain, hey! |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Silver! There it goes, Silver! |
| PROSPERO | Fury, Fury! There, [[archetypes/tyrant\|Tyrant]], there! Hark, hark! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Stefano, [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], and [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]], pursued by spirits |
| PROSPERO | (To [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]) Go, charge my goblins that they grind their<br>joints<br>With dry convulsions, shorten up their sinews<br>With aged cramps, and more pinch-spotted make<br>them<br>Than pard or cat o’mountain.<br>Cries within |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Hark, they roar! |
| PROSPERO | Let them be hunted soundly. At this hour<br>Lies at my [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] all mine enemies.<br>Shortly shall all my labours end, and thou<br>Shalt have the air at [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]]. For a little,<br>Follow, and do me service. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prospero, in his magic robes, and [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] |
| PROSPERO | Now does my project gather to a head.<br>My charms crack not, my spirits obey, and [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>Goes upright with his carriage. How’s the day? |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | On the sixth hour; at which [[motifs/time\|time]], my lord,<br>You said our work should cease. |
| PROSPERO | I did say so<br>When first I raised the tempest. Say, my spirit,<br>How fares the [[archetypes/king\|King]] and’s followers? |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Confined together<br>In the same fashion as you gave in charge,<br>Just as you left them; all prisoners, sir,<br>In the lime-grove which weather-fends your cell.<br>They cannot budge till your release. The [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>His brother, and yours, abide all three distracted,<br>And the remainder mourning over them,<br>Brimful of sorrow and dismay; but chiefly<br>Him that you termed, sir, the good old lord [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]]:<br>His [[motifs/tears\|tears]] run down his beard like [[motifs/winter\|winter]]’s drops<br>From eaves of reeds. Your charm so strongly works ’em<br>That if you now beheld them your affections<br>Would become tender. |
| PROSPERO | Dost thou think so, spirit? |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Mine would, sir, were I human. |
| PROSPERO | And mine shall.<br>Hast thou, which art but air, a touch, a feeling<br>Of their afflictions, and shall not myself,<br>One of their kind, that relish all as sharply<br>Passion as they, be kindlier moved than thou art?<br>Though with their high wrongs I am struck to th’<br>quick,<br>Yet with my nobler reason ’gainst my fury<br>Do I take part. The rarer action is<br>In virtue than in vengeance. They being penitent,<br>The sole drift of my purpose doth extend<br>Not a frown further. Go release them, [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]].<br>My charms I’ll break, their senses I’ll restore,<br>And they shall be themselves. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | I’ll fetch them, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Prospero draws a circle with his staff |
| PROSPERO | Ye elves of hills, brooks, standing lakes and groves,<br>And ye that on the sands with printless foot<br>Do chase the ebbing Neptune, and do fly him<br>When he comes back; you demi-puppets that<br>By moonshine do the green sour ringlets make<br>Whereof the ewe not bites; and you whose pastime<br>Is to make midnight mushrooms, that rejoice<br>To hear the solemn curfew; by whose aid,<br>Weak masters though ye be, I have bedimmed<br>The noontide sun, called forth the mutinous winds,<br>And ‘twixt the green sea and the azured vault<br>Set roaring war—to the dread rattling thunder<br>Have I given [[motifs/fire\|fire]], and rifted Jove’s stout oak<br>With his own bolt; the strong-based promontory<br>Have I made shake, and by the spurs plucked up<br>The pine and cedar; graves at my command<br>Have waked their sleepers, oped, and let ’em forth<br>By my so potent art. But this rough magic<br>I here abjure. And when I have required<br>Some heavenly music—which even now I do—<br>To work mine end upon their senses that<br>This airy charm is for, I’ll break my staff,<br>Bury it certain fathoms in the earth,<br>And deeper than did ever plummet sound<br>I’ll drown my book.<br>Solemn music. Here enters first [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], invisible; then [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], with a frantic gesture, attended by [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]]; [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]], in like manner, attended by Adrian and Francisco. They all enter the circle which Prospero had made, and there stand charmed; which Prospero observing, speaks<br>(To [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]]) A solemn air, and the best comforter<br>To an unsettled fancy, cure thy brains,<br>Now useless, boiled within thy skull.<br>(To [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) There stand,<br>For you are spelt-stopped.—<br>Holy [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]], honourable man,<br>Mine eyes, ev’n sociable to the show of thine,<br>Fall fellowly drops. (Aside) The charm dissolves apace,<br>And as the morning steals upon the [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>Melting the darkness, so their rising senses<br>Begin to chase the ignorant fumes that mantle<br>Their clearer reason.—O good [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]],<br>My true preserver, and a loyal sir<br>To him thou follow’st, I will pay thy graces<br>[[motifs/home\|Home]] both in word and deed.—Most cruelly<br>Didst thou, [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], use me and my daughter.<br>Thy brother was a furtherer in the act.—<br>Thou art pinched for’t now, [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]].<br>(To [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) Flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>You, brother mine, that entertained [[concepts/ambition\|ambition]],<br>Expelled remorse and [[concepts/nature\|nature]], whom, with [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]]—<br>Whose inward pinches therefore are most strong,—<br>Would here have killed your [[archetypes/king\|king]], I do forgive thee,<br>Unnatural though thou art. (Aside) Their understanding<br>Begins to swell, and the approaching tide<br>Will shortly fill the reasonable shores<br>That now lie foul and muddy. Not one of them<br>That yet looks on me, or would know me.—[[characters/ariel\|Ariel]],<br>Fetch me the hat and rapier in my cell.<br>I will disease me, and myself present<br>As I was sometime Milan. Quickly, spirit!<br>Thou shalt ere long be free.<br>[[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] sings and helps to attire him as [[characters/duke-of-milan\|Duke of Milan]] |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | Where the bee sucks, there suck I: In a cowslip’s bell I lie;<br>There I couch when owls do cry.<br>On the bat’s back I do fly<br>After [[motifs/summer\|summer]] merrily.<br>Merrily, merrily shall I live now<br>Under the blossom that hangs on the bough.<br>Merrily, merrily shall I live now<br>Under the blossom that hangs on the bough. |
| PROSPERO | Why, that’s my dainty [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]! I shall miss thee,<br>But yet thou shalt have [[concepts/freedom\|freedom]].—So, so, so.—<br>To the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s [[motifs/ship\|ship]], invisible as thou art!<br>There shalt thou find the mariners asleep<br>Under the hatches. The Master and the Boatswain<br>Being awake, enforce them to this place,<br>And presently, I prithee. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | I drink the air before me, and return<br>Or ere your pulse twice beat. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | All torment, trouble, [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]], and amazement<br>Inhabits here. Some heavenly power guide us<br>Out of this fearful country! |
| PROSPERO | Behold, sir [[archetypes/king\|King]],<br>The wronged [[characters/duke-of-milan\|Duke of Milan]], Prospero.<br>For more assurance that a living prince<br>Does now speak to thee, I embrace thy [[concepts/body\|body]];<br>And to thee and thy company I bid<br>A hearty welcome.<br>He embraces [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]] |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Whe’er thou beest he or no,<br>Or some enchanted trifle to abuse me,<br>As late I have been, I not know. Thy pulse<br>Beats as of flesh and [[motifs/blood\|blood]]; and since I saw thee<br>Th’affliction of my mind amends, with which<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] a [[concepts/madness\|madness]] held me. This must crave—<br>An if this be at all—a most strange story.<br>Thy dukedom I resign, and do entreat<br>Thou pardon me my wrongs. But how should<br>Prospero<br>Be living and be here? |
| PROSPERO | (to [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]])<br>First, noble friend,<br>Let me embrace thine age, whose [[concepts/honour\|honour]] cannot<br>Be measured or confined.<br>He embraces [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]] |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Whether this be<br>Or be not, I’ll not swear. |
| PROSPERO | You do yet taste<br>Some subtleties o’th’ isle that will not let you<br>Believe things certain.—Welcome, my friends all.<br>(Aside to [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] and [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]])<br><br>But you, my brace of lords, were I so minded,<br>I here could pluck his highness’ frown upon you<br>And justify you traitors. At this [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I will tell no tales. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | (to [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) The devil speaks in him. |
| PROSPERO | No.<br>(To [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]) For you, most wicked sir, whom to call<br>brother<br>Would even infect my mouth, I do forgive<br>Thy rankest fault, all of them, and require<br>My dukedom of thee, which perforce I know<br>Thou must restore. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | If thou beest Prospero,<br>Give us particulars of thy preservation,<br>How thou hast met us here, whom three hours since<br>Were wrecked upon this shore, where I have lost—<br>How sharp the point of this remembrance is!—<br>My dear son [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]]. |
| PROSPERO | I am woe for’t, sir. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Irreparable is the loss, and patience<br>Says it is past her cure. |
| PROSPERO | I rather think<br>You have not sought her help, of whose soft grace<br>For the like loss I have her sovereign aid,<br>And rest myself content. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | You the like loss? |
| PROSPERO | As great to me as late; and supportable<br>To make the dear loss have I means much weaker<br>Than you may call to comfort you, for I<br>Have lost my daughter. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | A daughter?<br>O heavens, that they were living both in Naples,<br>The [[archetypes/king\|king]] and [[characters/queen\|queen]] there! That they were, I wish<br>Myself were mudded in that oozy bed<br>Where my son lies. When did you lose your daughter? |
| PROSPERO | In this last tempest. I perceive these lords<br>At this encounter do so much admire<br>That they devour their reason, and scarce think<br>Their eyes do offices of [[concepts/truth\|truth]], these words<br>Are natural breath. But howsoe’er you have<br>Been jostled from your senses, know for certain<br>That I am Prospero, and that very Duke<br>Which was thrust forth of Milan, who most strangely,<br>Upon this shore where you were wrecked, was landed<br>To be the lord on’t. No more yet of this,<br>For ’tis a chronicle of day by day,<br>Not a relation for a breakfast, nor<br>Befitting this first meeting. Welcome, sir.<br>This cell’s my [[settings/court\|court]]. Here have I few attendants,<br>And subjects none abroad. Pray you, look in.<br>My dukedom since you have given me again,<br>I will requite you with as good a thing;<br>At least bring forth a [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]] to content ye<br>As much as me my dukedom.<br>Here Prospero discovers [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]], playing at chess |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | Sweet lord, you play me false. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | No, my dearest [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>I would not for the world. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | Yes, for a score of kingdoms you should wrangle,<br>An I would call it fair play. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | If this prove<br>A vision of the island, one dear son<br>Shall I twice lose. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | A most high miracle. |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | (coming forward)<br>Though the seas threaten, they are merciful.<br>I have cursed them without cause.<br>He kneels |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Now all the blessings<br>Of a glad father compass thee about.<br>Arise and say how thou cam’st here.<br>[[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] rises |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | (coming forward)<br>O [[concepts/wonder\|wonder]]!<br>How many goodly creatures are there here!<br>How beauteous mankind is! O brave new world<br>That has such people in’t! |
| PROSPERO | ’Tis new to thee. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | (to [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]])<br>What is this maid with whom thou wast at play?<br>Your eld’st acquaintance cannot be three hours.<br>Is she the goddess that hath severed us,<br>And brought us thus together? |
| [[characters/ferdinand\|FERDINAND]] | Sir, she is mortal;<br>But by immortal [[concepts/providence\|providence]] she’s mine.<br>I chose her when I could not ask my father<br>For his advice, nor thought I had one. She<br>Is daughter to this famous [[characters/duke-of-milan\|Duke of Milan]],<br>Of whom so often I have heard renown,<br>But never saw before; of whom I have<br>Received a second life; and second father<br>This lady makes him to me. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | I am hers.<br>But O, how oddly will it sound, that I<br>Must ask my [[archetypes/child\|child]] [[concepts/forgiveness\|forgiveness]]! |
| PROSPERO | There, sir, stop.<br>Let us not burden our remembrance with<br>A heaviness that’s gone. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | I have inly wept,<br>Or should have spoke ere this. Look down, you gods,<br>And on this couple drop a blessed [[motifs/crown\|crown]],<br>For it is you that have chalked forth the way<br>Which brought us hither. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | I say amen, [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]]. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Was Milan thrust from Milan, that his issue<br>Should become kings of Naples? O rejoice<br>Beyond a common joy! And set it down<br>With gold on lasting pillars: in one voyage<br>Did Claribel her husband find at Tunis,<br>And [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] her brother found a wife<br>Where he himself was lost; Prospero his dukedom<br>In a poor isle; and all of us ourselves,<br>When no man was his own. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | (to [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]] and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]]) Give me your hands.<br>Let grief and sorrow still embrace his heart<br>That doth not wish you joy. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Be it so! Amen! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], with the Master and Boatswain amazedly following |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | O look, sir, look, sir, here is more of us!<br>I prophesied if a gallows were on land<br>This fellow could not drown. (To the Boatswain) Now,<br><br>blasphemy,<br>That swear’st grace o’erboard: not an oath on shore?<br>Hast thou no mouth by land? What is the news? |
| BOATSWAIN | The best news is that we have safely found<br>Our [[archetypes/king\|King]] and company. The next, our [[motifs/ship\|ship]],<br>Which but three glasses since we gave out split,<br>Is tight and yare and bravely rigged, as when<br>We first put out to sea. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | (aside to Prospero) Sir, all this service<br>Have I done since I went. |
| PROSPERO | (aside to [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]])<br>My tricksy spirit! |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | These are not natural events; they strengthen<br>From strange to stranger. Say, how came you hither? |
| BOATSWAIN | If I did think, sir, I were [[motifs/well\|well]] awake<br>I’d strive to tell you. We were dead of [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]],<br>And—how we know not—all clapped under hatches,<br>Where but even now, with strange and several noises<br>Of roaring, shrieking, howling, jingling [[motifs/chains\|chains]],<br>And more diversity of sounds, all horrible,<br>We were awaked; straightway at liberty;<br>Where we in all her trim freshly beheld<br>Our royal, good, and gallant [[motifs/ship\|ship]], our Master<br>Cap’ring to eye her. On a trice, so please you,<br>Even in a dream, were we divided from them,<br>And were brought moping hither. |
| [[characters/ariel\|ARIEL]] | (aside to Prospero)<br>Was’t [[motifs/well\|well]] done? |
| PROSPERO | (aside to [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]])<br>Bravely, my diligence. Thou shalt be free. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | This is as strange a maze as e’er men trod,<br>And there is in this business more than [[concepts/nature\|nature]]<br>Was ever conduct of. Some oracle<br>Must rectify our knowledge. |
| PROSPERO | Sir, my liege,<br>Do not infest your mind with beating on<br>The strangeness of this business. At picked leisure,<br>Which shall be shortly, single I’ll resolve you,<br>Which to you shall seem probable, of every<br>These happened accidents; till when be cheerful,<br>And think of each thing [[motifs/well\|well]]. (Aside to [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]) Come<br>hither, spirit.<br>Set [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]] and his companions free.<br>Untie the spell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] |
| PROSPERO | (To [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]]) How fares my gracious sir?<br>There are yet missing of your company<br>Some few odd lads that you remember not. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], driving in [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]], Stefano, and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]], in their stolen apparel |
| STEFANO | Every man shift for all the rest, and let no man take care for himself, for all is but fortune. Coragio, bully-[[archetypes/monster\|monster]], coragio! |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | If these be true spies which I wear in my head, here’s a goodly sight. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | O Setebos, these be brave spirits indeed!<br>How fine my master is! I am afraid<br>He will chastise me. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Ha, ha! What things are these, my lord [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]]?<br>Will money buy ’em? |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Very like; one of them<br>Is a plain fish, and no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] marketable. |
| PROSPERO | Mark but the badges of these men, my lords,<br>Then say if they be true. This misshapen knave,<br>His mother was a [[characters/witch\|witch]], and one so strong<br>That could control the [[motifs/moon\|moon]], make flows and ebbs,<br>And deal in her command without her power.<br>These three have robbed me, and this demi-devil,<br>For he’s a bastard one, had plotted with them<br>To take my life. Two of these fellows you<br>Must know and own. This thing of darkness I<br>Acknowledge mine. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | I shall be pinched to [[motifs/death\|death]]. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Is not this Stefano, my drunken butler? |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | He is drunk now. Where had he wine? |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | And [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]] is reeling ripe. Where should they<br>Find this grand liquor that hath gilded ’em?<br>(To [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]]) How cam’st thou in this pickle? |
| [[characters/trinculo\|TRINCULO]] | I have been in such a pickle since I saw you last that, I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] me, will never out of my bones. I shall not [[concepts/fear\|fear]] fly-blowing. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Why, how now, Stefano? |
| STEFANO | O, touch me not! I am not Stefano, but a cramp. |
| PROSPERO | You’d be [[archetypes/king\|king]] o’the isle, sirrah? |
| STEFANO | I should have been a sore one, then. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | (pointing to [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) This is a strange thing as e’er I looked on. |
| PROSPERO | He is as disproportioned in his manners<br>As in his shape. (To [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]]) Go, sirrah, to my cell.<br>Take with you your companions. As you look<br>To have my pardon, trim it handsomely. |
| [[characters/caliban\|CALIBAN]] | Ay, that I will; and I’ll be wise hereafter,<br>And seek for grace. What a thrice-[[topoi/double\|double]] ass<br>Was I to take this drunkard for a god,<br>And worship this dull fooll |
| PROSPERO | Go to, away! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/caliban\|Caliban]] |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | (to Stefano and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]])<br>Hence, and bestow your luggage where you found it. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Or stole it, rather. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Stefano and [[characters/trinculo\|Trinculo]] |
| PROSPERO | (to [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]])<br>Sir, I invite your highness and your train<br>To my poor cell, where you shall take your rest<br>For this one [[motifs/night\|night]]; which part of it I’ll waste<br>With such discourse as I not [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] shall make it<br>Go quick away: the story of my life,<br>And the particular accidents gone by<br>Since I came to this isle. And in the morn<br>I’ll bring you to your [[motifs/ship\|ship]], and so to Naples,<br>Where I have [[concepts/hope\|hope]] to see the nuptial<br>Of these our dear-belovèd solemnized;<br>And thence retire me to my Milan, where<br>Every third thought shall be my [[motifs/grave\|grave]]. |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | I long<br>To hear the story of your life, which must<br>Take the ear strangely. |
| PROSPERO | I’ll deliver all,<br>And promise you calm seas, auspicious gales,<br>And sail so expeditious that shall catch<br>Your royal fleet far off. (Aside to [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]]) My [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]], chick,<br>That is thy charge. Then to the elements<br>Be free, and fare thou [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/ariel\|Ariel]] |
| PROSPERO | Please you, draw near. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Prospero |
| PROSPERO | Epilogue |
| PROSPERO | Now my charms are all o’erthrown,<br>And what strength I have’s mine own,<br>Which is most faint. Now ’tis true<br>I must be here confined by you<br>Or sent to Naples. Let me not,<br>Since I have my dukedom got,<br>And pardoned the deceiver, dwell<br>In this bare island by your spell;<br>But release me from my bands<br>With the help of your good hands.<br>Gentle breath of yours my sails<br>Must fill, or else my project fails,<br>Which was to please. Now I want<br>Spirits to enforce, art to enchant;<br>And my ending is [[concepts/despair\|despair]]<br>Unless I be relieved by [[motifs/prayer\|prayer]],<br>Which pierces so, that it assaults<br>[[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]] itself, and frees all faults.<br>As you from crimes would pardoned be,<br>Let your indulgence set me free.<br>He awaits applause, then exit |
| CARDENIO | A BRIEF ACCOUNT |
| MANY | plays acted in Shakespeare’s [[motifs/time\|time]] have failed to survive; they may easily include some that he wrote. The mystery of [[motifs/love\|Love]]’s Labour’s Won is discussed elsewhere (pp. xxxvii, 337). Certain manuscript records of the seventeenth century suggest that at least one other play in which he had a hand may have disappeared. On 9 September 1653 the [[settings/london\|London]] publisher Humphrey Moseley entered in the Stationers’ Register a batch of plays including ‘The [[concepts/history\|History]] of Cardenio, by Mr Fletcher and Shakespeare’. Cardenio is a character in Part One of Cervantes’ [[characters/don-quixote\|Don Quixote]], published in English [[forms/translation\|translation]] in 1612. Two earlier allusions suggest that the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s Men owned a play on this subject at the [[motifs/time\|time]] that Shakespeare was collaborating with John Fletcher (1579-1625). On 20 May 1613 the Privy Council authorized payment of £20 to John Heminges, as leader of the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s Men, for the presentation at [[settings/court\|court]] of six plays, one listed as ‘Cardenno’. On 9 July of the same year Heminges received £6 13s. 4d. for his company’s performance of a play ‘called Cardenna’ before the ambassador of the Duke of Savoy.<br>No more information about this play survives from the seventeenth century, but in 1728 Lewis Theobald published a play based on the story of Cardenio and called [[topoi/double\|Double]] Falsehood, or The Distrest Lovers, which he claimed to have ‘revised and adapted’ from one ‘written originally by W. Shakespeare’. It had been successfully produced at Drury Lane on 13 December 1727, and was given thirteen times up to 1 May 1728. Other performances are recorded in 1740, 1741, 1767 (when it was reprinted), 1770, and 1847. In 1770 a newspaper stated that ‘the original manuscript’ was ‘treasured up in the Museum of Covent [[motifs/garden\|Garden]] Playhouse’; [[motifs/fire\|fire]] destroyed the theatre, including its library, in 1808.<br>Theobald claimed to own several manuscripts of an original play by Shakespeare, and remarked that some of his contemporaries thought the style was Fletcher’s, not Shakespeare’s. When he himself came to edit Shakespeare’s plays he did not include either [[topoi/double\|Double]] Falsehood or the play on which he claimed to have based it; he simply edited the plays of the First Folio, not adding either [[characters/pericles\|Pericles]] or The Two Noble Kinsmen, though he believed they were partly by Shakespeare. It is quite possible that [[topoi/double\|Double]] Falsehood is based (however distantly) on a play of Shakespeare’s [[motifs/time\|time]]; if so, the play is likely to have been the one performed by the [[archetypes/king\|King]]’s Men and ascribed by Moseley in 1653 to Fletcher and Shakespeare.<br>[[topoi/double\|Double]] Falsehood is a [[forms/tragicomedy\|tragicomedy]]; the characters’ names differ from those in [[characters/don-quixote\|Don Quixote]], and the story is varied. Henriquez rapes Violante, then falls in [[motifs/love\|love]] with Leonora, loved by his friend Julio. Her parents agree to the [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]], but Julio interrupts the ceremony. Leonora (who had intended to kill herself) swoons and later takes sanctuary in a nunnery. Julio goes mad with [[motifs/desire\|desire]] for vengeance on his false friend; and the wronged Violante, disguised as a boy, joins a group of shepherds, and is almost raped by one of them. Henriquez’s virtuous brother, Roderick, ignorant of his villainy, helps him to abduct Leonora. Leonora and Violante both denounce Henriquez to Roderick. Finally Henriquez repents and marries Violante, while Julio (now sane) marries Leonora.<br>Some of the motifs of [[topoi/double\|Double]] Falsehood, such as the disguised heroine wronged by her [[archetypes/lover\|lover]] and, particularly, the reuniting and [[concepts/reconciliation\|reconciliation]] of parents with children, recall Shakespeare’s late plays. But most of the dialogue seems un-Shakespearian. Though the play deserved its limited success, it is now no more than an interesting curiosity. |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="The Tempest — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/king,motif/death,motif/lightning,motif/love,motif/ship,motif/silence,motif/storm,motif/water,motif/wind,concept/mercy,setting/ship,character/alonso,character/antonio,character/ferdinand,character/gonzalo,character/sebastian"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | A tempestuous noise of thunder and [[motifs/lightning\|lightning]] heard. Enter severally a Shipmaster and a Boatswain |
| MASTER | Boatswain! |
| BOATSWAIN | Here, Master. What cheer? |
| MASTER | Good, speak to th’ mariners. Fall to’t yarely, or we run ourselves aground. Bestir, bestir! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Mariners |
| BOATSWAIN | Heigh, my hearts! Cheerly, cheerly, my hearts! Yare, yare! Take in the topsail! Tend to th’ Master’s whistte!—Blow till thou burst thy [[motifs/wind\|wind]], if room enough. Enter [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]], [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]], [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]], and others |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Good Boatswain, have care. Where’s the Master? (To the Mariners) Play the men! |
| BOATSWAIN | I pray now, keep below. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Where is the Master, Boatswain? |
| BOATSWAIN | Do you not hear him? You mar our labour. Keep your cabins; you do assist the [[motifs/storm\|storm]]. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Nay, good, be patient. |
| BOATSWAIN | When the sea is. Hence! What cares these roarers for the name of [[archetypes/king\|king]]? To cabin! [[motifs/silence\|Silence]]; trouble us not. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Good, yet remember whom thou hast aboard. |
| BOATSWAIN | None that I more [[motifs/love\|love]] than myself. You are a councillor; if you can command these elements to [[motifs/silence\|silence]] and work peace of the present, we will not hand a rope more. Use your authority. If you cannot, give thanks you have lived so long and make yourself ready in your cabin for the mischance of the hour, if it so hap. (To the Mariners) Cheerly, good hearts! (To [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]]) Out of our way, I say! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | I have great comfort from this fellow. Methinks he hath no drowning mark upon him; his complexion is perfect gallows. Stand fast, good Fate, to his hanging. Make the rope of his destiny our cable, for our own doth little advantage. If he be not born to be hanged, our case is miserable. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Courtiers |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Boatswain |
| BOATSWAIN | Down with the topmast! Yare! Lower, lower! Bring her to try wi’th’ main-course!<br>A cry within<br>A plague upon this howling! They are louder than the weather, or our office. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]], and [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]] |
| BOATSWAIN | Yet again? What do you here? Shall we give o’er and drown? Have you a mind to sink? |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | A pox o’your throat, you bawling, blasphemous, incharitable dog! |
| BOATSWAIN | Work you, then. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Hang, cur, hang, you whoreson insolent noisemaker. We are less afraid to be drowned than thou art. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Mariners |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | I’ll warrant him for drowning, though the [[motifs/ship\|ship]] were no stronger than a nutshell and as leaky as an unstanched wench. |
| BOATSWAIN | Lay her a-hold, a-hold! Set her two courses! Off to sea again! Lay her off! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Mariners, wet |
| MARINERS | All lost! To prayers, to prayers! All lost! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Mariners |
| BOATSWAIN | What, must our mouths be cold? |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | The [[archetypes/king\|King]] and Prince at prayers! Let’s assist them,<br>For our case is as theirs. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | I’m out of patience. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | We are merely cheated of our lives by drunkards.<br>This wide-chopped rascal—would thou mightst lie<br>drowning<br>The washing of ten tides. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | He’ll be hanged yet,<br>Though every drop of [[motifs/water\|water]] swear against it<br>And gape at wid’st to glut him.<br>A confused noise within |
| MARINERS | (within)<br>[[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]] on us!<br>We split, we split! Farewell, my wife and children!<br>Farewell, brother! We split, we split, we spht! |
| *(didascalia)* | Axit Boatswain |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Let’s all sink wi’th’ [[archetypes/king\|King]]. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Let’s take leave of him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] and [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Now would I give a thousand furlongs of sea for an acre of barren ground: long heath, broom, furze, anything. The wills above be done, but I would fain die a dry [[motifs/death\|death]]. Exit |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Si ode un fragore tempestoso di tuono e di [[motifs/lightning\|lampi]]. Entrano da parti opposte un Capitano di nave e un Nostromo |
| CAPITANO | Nostromo! |
| NOSTROMO | Son qui, Capitano. Che c'è di buono? |
| CAPITANO | Bene, parla ai marinai. Datevi da fare, e in fretta, o finiamo in secca. Muovetevi, muovetevi! |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano i Marinai |
| NOSTROMO | Ehi, cuori miei! Animo, animo, cuori miei! Svelti, svelti! Ammainate la gabbia! Attenti al fischio del Capitano!—Soffia fino a scoppiarne il tuo [[motifs/wind\|vento]], se c'è spazio abbastanza. Entrano [[characters/alonso\|Alonso]], [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]], [[characters/ferdinand\|Ferdinand]], [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]] e altri |
| [[characters/alonso\|ALONSO]] | Buon Nostromo, abbi cura. Dov'è il Capitano? (Ai marinai) Fate da uomini! |
| NOSTROMO | Vi prego, ora, restate sottocoperta. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Dov'è il Capitano, Nostromo? |
| NOSTROMO | Non lo sentite? Ci guastate la fatica. State nelle vostre cabine: date man forte alla [[motifs/storm\|tempesta]]. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Suvvia, buon uomo, sii paziente. |
| NOSTROMO | Quando lo sarà il mare. Via di qui! Che importa a questi ruggenti del nome di [[archetypes/king\|re]]? In cabina! [[motifs/silence\|Silenzio]]; non ci disturbate. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Bene, ma ricordati chi hai a bordo. |
| NOSTROMO | Nessuno che io [[motifs/love\|ami]] più di me stesso. Voi siete consigliere; se sapete comandare a questi elementi il [[motifs/silence\|silenzio]] e far pace col momento presente, non toccheremo più una cima. Usate la vostra autorità. Se non potete, ringraziate d'aver vissuto tanto a lungo e preparatevi nella vostra cabina alla sventura dell'ora, se mai debba venire. (Ai marinai) Animo, bravi cuori! (A [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]]) Fuori dai piedi, dico! |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Da costui traggo gran conforto. Mi pare che non abbia addosso alcun marchio d'annegato; la sua cera è perfetto patibolo. Tieni fermo, buon Destino, alla sua impiccagione. Fa' della corda del suo destino la nostra gomena, ché la nostra ci giova poco. Se non è nato per essere impiccato, il nostro caso è miserevole. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i Cortigiani |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra il Nostromo |
| NOSTROMO | Giù l'albero di gabbia! Svelti! Più giù, più giù! Mettetela alla cappa con la maestra!<br>Un grido dall'interno<br>La peste su questi urli! Son più forti del tempo, e del nostro lavoro. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]], [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] e [[characters/gonzalo\|Gonzalo]] |
| NOSTROMO | Ancora? Che fate qui? Dobbiamo lasciar tutto e annegare? Avete voglia di colare a picco? |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Il canchero alla tua gola, cane urlatore, bestemmiatore, spietato! |
| NOSTROMO | Lavorate voi, allora. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Impiccati, botolo, impiccati, insolente schiamazzatore figlio di puttana. Abbiamo meno paura d'annegare di quanta ne hai tu. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i Marinai |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Garantisco io che non annegherà, foss'anche la [[motifs/ship\|nave]] non più salda d'un guscio di noce e sconnessa come una femmina che non si tura. |
| NOSTROMO | Tenetela al vento, al vento! Spiegate le due vele basse! Di nuovo al largo! Portatela fuori! |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano i Marinai, fradici |
| MARINAI | Tutto perduto! Alle preghiere, alle preghiere! Tutto perduto! |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i Marinai |
| NOSTROMO | Come, dovranno restar fredde le nostre bocche? |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Il [[archetypes/king\|Re]] e il Principe in preghiera! Andiamo ad assisterli,<br>ché il nostro caso è come il loro. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Non ho più pazienza. |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Ci vengon rubate le vite, e solo da ubriaconi.<br>Questo furfante dalle larghe mascelle—potessi tu giacere<br>annegato<br>al lavacro di dieci maree. |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Sarà impiccato lo stesso,<br>anche se ogni goccia d'[[motifs/water\|acqua]] giurasse il contrario<br>e spalancasse la bocca per inghiottirlo.<br>Un rumore confuso dall'interno |
| MARINAI | (dall'interno)<br>[[concepts/mercy\|Pietà]] di noi!<br>Ci spacchiamo, ci spacchiamo! Addio, moglie e figli miei!<br>Addio, fratello! Ci spacchiamo, ci spacchiamo, ci spacchiamo! |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce il Nostromo |
| [[characters/antonio\|ANTONIO]] | Affondiamo tutti insieme al [[archetypes/king\|Re]]. |
| [[characters/sebastian\|SEBASTIAN]] | Andiamo a congedarci da lui. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono [[characters/antonio\|Antonio]] e [[characters/sebastian\|Sebastian]] |
| [[characters/gonzalo\|GONZALO]] | Ora darei mille miglia di mare per un acro di terra sterile: brughiera alta, ginestra, ulice, qualunque cosa. Sia fatta la volontà del cielo, ma vorrei pur morire d'una [[motifs/death\|morte]] asciutta. Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2" data-title="The Tempest — Act I, Scene 2" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,archetype/villain,motif/bees,motif/crown,motif/fire,motif/frost,motif/grave,motif/home,motif/lightning,motif/love,motif/memory,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/prayer,motif/prison,motif/ship,motif/sleep,motif/soul,motif/storm,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/ambition,concept/beauty,concept/charity,concept/conscience,concept/doubt,concept/faith,concept/fear,concept/guilt,concept/honour,concept/mercy,concept/nature,concept/providence,concept/self,concept/truth,concept/wonder,histref/inquisition,setting/court,setting/ship,character/antonio,character/ariel,character/caliban,character/duke_of_milan,character/ferdinand,character/gonzalo,character/miranda,character/queen,character/witch"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Prospero in his magic cloak, with a staff, and [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | If by your art, my dearest father, you have<br>Put the wild waters in this roar, allay them.<br>The sky, it seems, would pour down stinking pitch,<br>But that the sea, mounting to th’ welkin’s cheek,<br>Dashes the [[motifs/fire\|fire]] out. O, I have suffered<br>With those that I saw suffer! A brave vessel,<br>Who had, no [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]], some noble creature in her,<br>Dashed all to pieces! O, the cry did knock<br>Against my very heart! Poor souls, they perished.<br>Had I been any god of power, I would<br>Have sunk the sea within the earth, or ere<br>It should the good [[motifs/ship\|ship]] so have swallowed and<br>The fraughting souls within her. |
| PROSPERO | Be collected.<br>No more amazement. Tell your piteous heart<br>There’s no harm done. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | O woe the day! |
| PROSPERO | No harm.<br>I have done nothing but in care of thee,<br>Of thee, my dear one, thee, my daughter, who<br>Art ignorant of what thou art, naught knowing<br>Of whence I am, nor that I am more better<br>Than Prospero, master of a full poor cell<br>And thy no greater father. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | More to know<br>Did never meddle with my thoughts. |
| PROSPERO | ’Tis [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>I should inform thee farther. Lend thy hand,<br>And pluck my magic garment from me. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] removes Prospero’s cloak, and he lays it |
| *(didascalia)* | on the ground |
| PROSPERO | So.<br>Lie there, my art.—Wipe thou thine eyes; have comfort.<br>The direful spectacle of the wreck, which touched<br>The very virtue of compassion in thee,<br>I have with such provision in mine art<br>So safely ordered that there is no [[motifs/soul\|soul]]—<br>No, not so much perdition as an hair<br>Betid to any creature in the vessel,<br>Which thou heard‘st cry, which thou saw’st sink. Sit<br>down,<br>For thou must now know farther.<br>[[characters/miranda\|Miranda]] sits |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | You have often<br>Begun to tell me what I am, but stopped<br>And left me to a bootless [[historical-references/inquisition\|inquisition]],<br>Concluding ‘Stay; not yet’. |
| PROSPERO | The hour’s now come.<br>The very minute bids thee ope thine ear,<br>Obey, and be attentive. Canst thou remember<br>A [[motifs/time\|time]] before we came unto this cell?<br>I do not think thou canst, for then thou wast not<br>Out three years old. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | Certainly, sir, I can. |
| PROSPERO | By what? By any other house or person?<br>Of anything the image tell me that<br>Hath kept with thy remembrance. |
| [[characters/miranda\|MIRANDA]] | ’Tis far off,<br>And rather like a dream than an assurance<br>That my remembrance warrants. Had I not<br>Four or five women once that tended me? |
